# Unit 6 linguistic and owned-edition mapping packet

This packet prepares review. It records an unsigned structural mapping proposal. It does not record a qualified review, an approved edition alignment, or an install decision.

The owned-edition mapping worksheet separates observed edition facts, the proposed lesson and printed page range, unresolved edition questions, and matters that still require qualified linguistic review. ISBN, owned-copy verification, variance, reviewer identity, qualification, date, and approval stay blank. The disposition remains PENDING_CURRICULUM_REVIEWER.

Unit 6 remains in development. Its readiness is content-complete and review-pending. The Lesson 6 relationship is a proposed, unsigned mapping. It is not an approved edition alignment.

`unitContentReviewDigest` binds this unit's instructional curriculum. It covers this unit and the reachable instructional records, relationships, shared dependencies, sources, rights, assets, and advancement rule. It does not cover any other unit. Recording a review status, reviewer signature, mapping approval, install state, or readiness does not change it.

`mappingPackageDigest` binds the proposed edition-mapping facts for Unit 6: edition identity, lesson, printed page range, topic description, observation source, ISBN observation state, and the pending disposition. Reviewer identity, qualification, date, notes, correction notes, and signature stay outside that digest.

`packContentReviewDigest` is the repository aggregate of instructional curriculum. It is not an approval of this unit, and it is not part of the review-package digest.

`reviewPackageDigest` identifies this complete pending review package. It binds the ordered instructional records and the mapping-package digest. Changing a proposed page range or topic changes the mapping-package digest and therefore this package digest.

`repositorySourceDigest` binds repository and release inputs. Recording an approval later changes that repository hash. That change does not replace the signed content-review digest or the mapping-package digest.

The marker scan used by this package detects only a fixed list of known prohibited mapping-input markers. It is not comprehensive copyright detection and cannot recognize every copy or close paraphrase of protected material.

recordType=meaning
sourceUrl=https://en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Russian/Grammar/Accusative_case?oldid=4330404
sourceRevision=4330404
sourceRecordId=accusative:The other use of the accusative case is with two major prepositions: '''в''' and
originalSourceText=The other use of the accusative case is with two major prepositions: '''в''' and '''на'''. When followed by the accusative case, '''в''' means 'to' or 'into', as in 'I walked ''into'' the house' ('''Я шёл в дом'''), and '''на''' means 'onto' or 'toward', as in 'I walked ''onto'' the street' ('''Я шёл на улицу'''). However, both prepositions can also use the [[Russian/Grammar/Prepositional case|prepositional case]]: '''в''' + ''prep'' means 'in' or 'at', as in 'I lived in London' ('''Я жил в Лондоне'''), and '''на''' + ''prep'' means 'upon' or 'at', as in 'It's on the table' ('''Оно на столе'''). The accusative and prepositional uses are similar, though generally the accusative case denotes ''direction'' ('I walked ''towards'' the hotel'), while the prepositional case denotes ''location'' ('I stayed ''at'' this hotel').
shippedText=the house
transformation=selection
licenseId=CC BY-SA 4.0
licenseUrl=https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/
attribution=Wikibooks Russian grammar contributors, CC BY-SA 4.0. Page history for each frozen oldid identifies the contributors.
shareAlikeTreatment=Adapted curriculum data, selection, and arrangement are distributed under CC BY-SA 4.0. Application code, user data, and user recordings are outside that statement. No audio is relicensed because this unit ships no audio.

recordType=instruction
sourceUrl=https://en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Russian/Grammar/Accusative_case?oldid=4330404
sourceRevision=4330404
sourceRecordId=accusative:The other use of the accusative case is with two major prepositions: '''в''' and
originalSourceText=The other use of the accusative case is with two major prepositions: '''в''' and '''на'''. When followed by the accusative case, '''в''' means 'to' or 'into', as in 'I walked ''into'' the house' ('''Я шёл в дом'''), and '''на''' means 'onto' or 'toward', as in 'I walked ''onto'' the street' ('''Я шёл на улицу'''). However, both prepositions can also use the [[Russian/Grammar/Prepositional case|prepositional case]]: '''в''' + ''prep'' means 'in' or 'at', as in 'I lived in London' ('''Я жил в Лондоне'''), and '''на''' + ''prep'' means 'upon' or 'at', as in 'It's on the table' ('''Оно на столе'''). The accusative and prepositional uses are similar, though generally the accusative case denotes ''direction'' ('I walked ''towards'' the hotel'), while the prepositional case denotes ''location'' ('I stayed ''at'' this hotel').
shippedText=generally the accusative case denotes ''direction''
transformation=selection
licenseId=CC BY-SA 4.0
licenseUrl=https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/
attribution=Wikibooks Russian grammar contributors, CC BY-SA 4.0. Page history for each frozen oldid identifies the contributors.
shareAlikeTreatment=Adapted curriculum data, selection, and arrangement are distributed under CC BY-SA 4.0. Application code, user data, and user recordings are outside that statement. No audio is relicensed because this unit ships no audio.

sourceRevision=4330404
sourceRecordId=accusative:For the third-person, the masculine and neuter possessive pronouns are the same 
originalSourceText=For the third-person, the masculine and neuter possessive pronouns are the same as the personal pronouns: '''его'''. The feminine is '''её''', and the third-person plural is '''их'''. These do not decline according to the gender or number of the modified noun.
shippedText=These do not decline according to the gender or number of the modified noun.
